No live account figure goes in this file or on any branch; live numbers travel by message only. Every figure below is a research result on public data, a rule or a cap. [research]

**PROBATION SEATS (the owner, popups relayed by the EA 19:55Z and 19:57Z; rules v1.1, section 5a).** His words: "im giving you full permissions on the strategy rotation ideas & deployments, with the one rule -- we need to hit our live minimum ASAP. by ASAP i mean probably before Monday morning. He chose "Probation seats, 3 contracts": up to 5 candidates live without a forward record, 3 contracts, at most $3 an order, an $18 drawdown kill each, stepping to 10 contracts only on the 60 plus 30 record. This reverses his "no orders before evidence" for probation seats only. Also decided: the favourite-late copies retired on Nov 11 stay declined; the slot caps go to their ceilings. **What the Head of R&D does and does not do under "full permissions":** ranks candidates, writes exact specs, keeps the rules and the ledger. It still places no order, merges nothing and deploys nothing; each probation seat goes to the owner as a popup with its exact spec and is built, reviewed and armed by the Chief of Staff. **Preliminary ranking sent to the EA at 20:00Z** (final with exact specs due 20:55Z): 1 CFRTI-K on Bitcoin (the only one with any outcome evidence, and weak); 2 and 3 DEPTH1 on Bitcoin and Ethereum (conditional on its read from Nov 14 00:00Z not being under 1.0); 4 ESOCTIE on e-soccer (no read yet); 5 REMATCH or MAPBREAK on CS2, whichever development read is better. A rule whose read comes back under 1.0 drops out. Honest expectation given to him: 2 or 3 probation seats by Monday morning if Friday's read is not negative; 5 needs the data pulls merged tonight, new live code for the sports rules and money on the right exchange shard. Downside by his lines: at most $18 a seat, $90 for five. Live spec sheets asked of Desk A, Desk B and the History Miner by 20:40Z.

**POLITICS ON THE WHEEL (the owner, through the EA 20:14Z: "politics, midterms, elections are all being considered right?").** Answer given in three lines: nothing from politics is on the wheel yet; in the queue are the politics recorder (42 series since Nov 11) and the combo house side (paper, Nov 26); closed are the approval and generic-ballot NO side (read, did not clear), POL-i2 (on cost, 9.4c round trip) and several ideas with too few independent events. Honest prior: politics is slow and none of this has evidence today.

**STANDING RULE (i), the full order toolkit (20:10Z; the owner, through the EA: "reminder -- we can use profit targets and stop losses and limit orders in our strategies.."):** every card is designed with a resting limit entry, a profit target, a stop and a time exit in view, as well as hold to settlement. A different exit or entry on a registered rule is a new try, registered before data, one variant per rule for a stated mechanical reason, no grids. A taker exit sells at the bid of the exit minute and pays the exit fee (round-trip cost kill above 5c); a stop fills at the first bid at or after it is crossed; resting orders are scored only where a book and a tape exist, by the through-price rule, with filled and unfilled orders both printed. Already known: holding beat every exit on the favourite family (EXIT1) and on OICORE's signal (SLOTEX); five resting-order tests failed on adverse fills; nothing has been tested on the sports and esports rules. Asked by 22:30Z: one line a rule from every author (a mechanical reason for a limit entry, target, stop or time exit, the one variant they would register, or "hold stays"); by 23:00Z a table from the Testing Desk of every rule that closed on cost, where a limit entry could change a verdict. The trade tape is being added to the wheel recorder's spec so resting entries can be scored on sports later. **The owner answered at 20:09Z, verbatim: "they dont always have to be hold to settlement".** Exits and limit entries are allowed in any rule; the old ban on short round trips is relaxed to one condition: every test counts both fees and the real spread on both legs and must clear the bar. nothing found showing a target, stop or trade-out beats holding; nothing found on fill rates at the mid or the bid. Step 2 waits for that amendment, the frozen hash and the auditor's CLEAN. **MAPBREAK audit (Auditor B, 00:04Z): two faults, both confirmed by the Head, both fixed before any result is read.** F1 (rule a): the scorer took the map state from the map market's result field, which is posted about 3 minutes after close, while the look is 2:00 to 3:00 after close; a live bot could not know it. Fix: the team taken is named only by Kalshi's own quote in a candle ending at or before the look (map contract best bid 0.90 or more, one-sided books allowed); otherwise the match is "state not observable at the look", counted and not a bet. F2 (fill): the signal selected on the look candle's quote and booked a fill at that same quote. Fix: a limit at the look candle's ask plus 1c, filled only if the next minute's candle shows an ask at or under the limit, paying the limit; otherwise "not filled", counted and printed; the same-candle figure is printed beside it labelled optimistic. One Amendment 7 (Desk B writes it, the Testing Desk changes the scorer, Forensics builds the second scorer from the amended text), labelled as made after step 1 counts and the audit and before any match result; the 150 minimum applies to filled bets; CS2 stays out as registered; tries stay at 2. The owner's popup comes once per rule, at admission with its exact spec; after that a rule takes a seat when its own forward record clears the bar, leaves on its kill lines, cools 14 days and may return on a fresh record. State is replayed from rows, never stored.
- **The entry bar and why:** a rule with no edge clears "1.3 over 60" on one block 8 percent of the time at 50c, 13 percent at 20c, 27 percent at 90c; re-read as it rolls, half to nine tenths of dead rules clear it sooner or later. With 30 rules watching, the bare rule seats about 3 lucky rules a month. So: forward signals only, a dated admit list, blocks that never overlap, 10 of the rarer outcome, a 30 signal confirmation (about 1 lucky seat a month across 30), fill-checked rows (a second look within 4 seconds), the longest record first when seats are short, no favourite-and-hold at 88c or above without the owner's ruling.
- **Kill lines (with Refinement's review):** $10 drawdown a rule; net under minus 1c a contract at 50 fills; the last 60 fills under 1.0 at every 60th fill; a $50 lane cap. A rolling 0.7 line is an alert only (on gold it fired mostly on a strategy that kept earning).
- **What it earns on coin flips (30 shadows, 5 seats, 3 contracts, about 4 signals a day a rule):** no rule real, about minus $2 to minus $5 a month with under 2 seats filled; 1 in 10 real, about plus $6 to plus $10; 1 in 5 real, about plus $21 with 2 to 3 seats filled. At 3 contracts it is a machine for finding what deserves scaling, not income by itself; five seats full at once needs more shadows, busier rules or a looser bar.
- **Feeding it:** 8 mechanisms have a written registered rule today (2 already live), about 10 to 12 in two weeks, 20 about January. 20 to 30 shadows within two weeks is possible only if one rule may hold a place on each market it runs on, which the owner's Nov 11 rule (lifecycle 7g) treats as a copy. - **Main's build plan:** about 4 days of logging and reporting (quote budget, OI and calendar fields, a rolling per-rule report with the fill check), then a replay-only controller, then live wiring only after the owner's OK and a security review.

## New cards tonight (the owner's "find more ideas"), as they arrive [research]

- **PREMOVE (Desk B, card 1 of up to 5; 453aa8cc on claude/idea-desk-b; 4 tries; registered 19:01Z before any price):** follow a pre-match move. If a contract's mid 5 minutes before the scheduled start (ticker clock) is 6c or more above its mid 65 minutes before, buy it at the next minute's ask plus 1c (ask 20c to 85c, spread 4c or less), hold to settlement; one bet a match. Families: esports, e-soccer, table tennis, e-basketball, about 3,200 to 3,600 development matches each. Named other side: resting orders and slower bettors who priced the match earlier. Its author's odds: about 3 percent that any family meets the development bar. Costs no requests of its own if MAPBREAK's and INPLAYJUMP's candle windows start 70 minutes before the scheduled start (approved). Cost kill is the one-way entry cost, as for every hold rule.
- **INPLAYJUMP, Amendment 1 (ordered 19:04Z, before any candle):** the candle window starts at a time known at the decision (ticker clock or market open) and runs forward, so "the first jump" cannot depend on when the match ended; a fired bet with no result is counted; the placebo is taken at a matched in-play minute. This corrects an earlier ruling of the Head of R&D that a close-anchored window was fine for retrieval.
- **ESOCTIE, NOTE 3 (History Miner, ec26e394, before any data):** a result enters the base rate only 300 seconds after that game's close (both auditors found it); one-way cost kill; void counts reported.
- **REMATCH (Desk B, card 2; 145354d7; 1 try; registered 19:12Z before any price):** e-soccer only. The listing shows about 450 matches a day between only about 53 pairs of players, and the next meeting of a pair is already open and trading while the earlier one is played. Rule, direction fixed before data (fade): when the same two players meet again and the previous loser's contract in the later market has fallen 5c or more since before the earlier match, buy that loser 5 minutes before the start at ask plus 1c (ask 20c to 80c, spread 4c or less), hold to settlement. Named other side: bettors chasing the last result and a maker's program that moves the next price with the last score. Accepted with changes before data: one-way cost kill (4c), the earlier result admitted only 300 seconds after its close, the single void rule, z also printed clustered by player-pair-day (the same few dozen players play all day, so independent events are nearer days than matches, and z may miss 5.46 even on a high profit factor). Stage 1 is counts and cost only on a slice already listed (about 110 candle requests, 0 tries); stage 2 (about 50 list pages and 440 candle requests) waits for the Head's go. About 1,200 development bets at a 10 percent share. Its author's odds: about 1.5 percent for the development bar.
- **PREMOVE cut from four families to two (19:13Z, before data):** e-soccer and e-basketball removed, because a late pre-match move there is mostly the market reacting to results just in, which is REMATCH's ground; no family is bet both ways. PREMOVE is esports and table tennis, 2 tries. **Correction by Desk B of its own figure:** PREMOVE is not free in requests; a wider candle window costs about 400 to 500 requests because starts are staggered. Approved up to 500, as its own later job, never delaying MAPBREAK's or INPLAYJUMP's candles. INPLAYJUMP's candle estimate rises from about 610 to about 900 to 1,000 for the same reason.

Dropped with the number: NFL high-total unders (59.7 percent in 2001 to 2009, 47.1 percent in 2010 to 2018), home underdogs (PF about 1.25), reverse longshot bias (below the vig), streak fading, draw bias, exchange studies (no bias on 200,000 matches), tennis rating models (about PF 1.07 at the best of 11 books' prices, negative at one book), the Kalshi papers (longshot bias only, which is banned; YES overpricing in mention markets gives PF 1.09 on average and is covered by MENT1 and MENTPERSIST1). No efficiency study exists for ITF tennis, table tennis or e-soccer.

## Cost kill corrected for hold-to-settlement rules, before any price was read (Nov 12, 19:02Z) [research]

Desk A's flag: the template cost kill "median round trip above 5c" (written by the Head of R&D) is right for a rule that exits before settlement (it closed POL-i2 correctly at 9.4c) and wrong for a rule that buys and holds: there is no second spread and no second fee, and with a 1-contract fee rounded up to 2c a side it would close every family whose spread is above 1c before one bet was scored. **Ruled, by dated amendment before any price is read, for INPLAYJUMP, MAPBREAK, ESOCTIE and SOCDERIV:** the cost kill is on the ONE-WAY entry cost at qualifying decision minutes, the median of (ask minus mid) plus the entry fee as charged; closed on cost if that median is above 4c, which equals each rule's own spread filter. It is a request-saving screen, not the bar: the scored PF pays the real ask, the 1c slip and the fee. The engine prints which cost line was used. INPLAYJUMP gets a five-request cost and tradeability pilot (no result read) before its full candle pull (about 610 requests); its lists job is 14 requests.

- **Auditor B (the former Stocks Desk) on INPLAYJUMP's engine at 86e801a2:** signal and entry leak-free, but the engine was not yet the registered rule (no in-play filter or bands, up to six bets a match, unrounded fee, no held-out guard, a placebo sharing the event's outcome). All to be conformed before any candle; the registration's clock guard is rewritten so nothing known only after the decision decides whether a bet exists (retrieval windows may use the close).
- **History Miner:** virtual sports has no mechanism beyond ESOCTIE, INPLAYJUMP and a rating idea already run (VIRT1); nothing else unclaimed in its table has 1,200 events in the cheap window. It now lists which families become readable in two, four and eight weeks as their seasons fill.

## CFRTI-K's "ran rows" line was an unsound stress test (Forensics, Nov 12 19:00Z, f1683c5) [research]

The stress line the Head of R&D specified for CFRTI-K ("ran rows dropped": drop a row when the next minute's ask is 1c or more higher) removes rows by a value measured after the decision that is partly the outcome arriving. It is biased against the rule and is not a fill test. On the 53 development rows where real R6 books exist: at the candle ask, 53 trades, PF 1.06; a 6 second order at the ask seen fills 38 of 53, PF 1.23 ("no evidence at n = 38"; eight winners decide every figure); at 2 seconds 37 of 53, PF 1.45. **This does not rescue CFRTI-K** (the registered line is PF 1.07 to 1.09 on about 600 trades, z about 0.6 to 0.7). The stated reason is corrected everywhere to "no edge shown near the bar", never "an edge that cannot be filled"; the owner was told. Block P is judged on the registered thirds alone (first third 0.95: not met). **Rule for every desk:** a "ran away" or "missed" haircut is never built from a later candle or any value measured after the decision; use the book replay where a book exists, otherwise a random drop at the measured miss rate with the caveat that real misses lean toward winners.

## Who reports to whom

Research reports to the Head of R&D. Engineering, merges, deploys, recorders, kalshi-pull and everything live are the Chief of Staff's (Main). The Verification Analyst is independent of research: any claimed pass or uniqueness score is re-checked through Main, never by the desk that produced it. The owner decides every popup.

## In-game lag, step 1 read (Desk B, Nov 12 17:25Z, zero tries; 3279566, section 11 of IDEA_DESK_B_INGAME_CARD.md) [research]

214 of 215 games used; 627 trigger events with a clean spread move; 131,774 in-play market-minutes with a usable quote. **Registered verdict: SURVIVES step 1** (420 of 627 events, 67 percent, had 75 percent of the spread's ten minute move in after one minute; the close line was 80 percent; NHL 79, NFL 56, NBA 51 percent). **The desk's own reading, accepted: this is not evidence of a lag.** The game goes on during the ten minutes, so a market with no lag would not score 100 percent and the 80 percent line was never calibrated; two selections in the script push the share down; the share does not climb from minute 1 to 3 (0.88, 0.89, 0.90), so any catching up is inside the first minute; NHL's spread is too costly anyway. Ruled: one extra number, defined in the card before being computed and labelled a post-read addition, may CLOSE the card (mean drift from minute 1 to 10 under the sport's round-trip cost in every sport) but cannot count as evidence; a sport above its cost goes to step 2 alone on my go. Step 2 is not run on the letter of the rule. ## OICORE's trigger is partly a price proxy (Nov 12, Forensics, public data)

ENTRYREPLAY1_OI (claude/forensics-desk, workers/forensics/ENTRYREPLAY1_OI_RESULT.md, 34f7698; the model's trigger windows on R6 books, not the bot's orders). Of 937 Bitcoin windows with a book at the look, 431 are trigger windows; in 87 percent of those the cheap side is at 8c or less, outside the band the rule may buy. Open interest is counted in contracts, so a rise of 86,300 is cheap to produce when one side is nearly worthless. Inside the 8c to 50c band the trigger still leans cheap (mean entry 18.5c against 26.1c for the rest of the band). So any comparison of the trigger with a baseline must be made within price buckets. Whether the trigger adds anything beyond price: no evidence at n = 14 on closes before Nov 5; about 100 in-band trigger windows will exist on books by early December (R6 now runs to Dec 15). Passed to Refinement for the Nov 13 forward read and Monday's report.

## Fill convention for taker rules (Nov 12, from Forensics' FILLHAIRCUT1)

Measured on recorded Bitcoin 15 minute books, closes Oct 29 to Nov 11, 180 to 120 seconds before the close, cheap side 5c to 30c: 9,527 decision instants in 544 windows (claude/forensics-desk, workers/forensics/FILLHAIRCUT1_RESULT.md, 448f5e2; no outcome loaded, zero tries; not yet checked by the Verification Analyst). Share of immediate-or-cancel buys filled in full at about 6 and 10 seconds: at the ask seen 66 percent; at +1c 76 and 73 percent; at +2c 82 and 78 percent. 1 and 10 contracts are the same. Fills are slightly cheaper than the ask seen, so no cents are added.

**The haircut at a live-like 5 to 10 seconds:** drop about 34 percent of simulated fills at the ask seen, 24 to 27 percent at +1c, 18 to 23 percent at +2c; keep the ask seen as the price.

**Two warnings.** The miss rate is not a constant (Nov 9 to 11 filled 4 to 7 points less). The dropped fills are not random: missed orders see the mid 8c to 11c higher 30 seconds later, filled ones 2c to 3c lower. For a rule whose signal predicts the next seconds these figures are a FLOOR.

**Convention:** every taker rule on the 15 minute markets is also reported as a replay on the recorded books at +2 and +6 seconds with its real limit; a miss is no trade; live use and probe gates are judged on the 6 second line. Where only the decision quote exists, apply the haircut above and show the stress that drops the best-outcome signals first. The CFRTI held-out plan carries the replay line (Desk A, 19c2751b and 541e83da on claude/idea-desk-a; no new try).

## The quote recorder is a delayed copy; the Bitcoin lead is probably an artifact (Nov 12)

Reference for every desk: kalshi-scalper/workers/forensics/DATA_SOURCES_FRESHNESS.md on claude/forensics-desk. Price from order books (book15r6/, nc15/, book/) or a trade tape. Treat any bid or ask that came out of a market list (quotes/, ladder.csv, the in-game, politics, daily, ref and combo quote files) as up to about 11 seconds old. Displayed book sizes are what was shown at that instant, never an upper limit. Not yet known: the single-market read, the perps list, the 1 minute candles (being checked).

Forensics' QUOTEVSBOOK1 (claude/forensics-desk, workers/forensics/QUOTEVSBOOK1_RESULT.md, registered 1539121, result 836d9fa; no outcome loaded, zero tries; Verification Analyst check requested first): the research quote recorder reads Kalshi's market LIST endpoint, which returns a copy refreshed about every 11 seconds (4,360 distinct quote states in 72,012 polls at 0.6 second spacing; median 10.96 seconds between changes), while the order book read by R6 never repeats between 2 second polls. Both clocks are right; the quote is 0 to 11 seconds old. As stamped, the quote is within 1c of the book on 57 percent of rows; it is below the book by 2c or more on 12.8 percent. Among the rows CFRTI picked that have a book, Desk A counts 5 of 16 three-venue and 4 of 12 Kraken-only with an ask more than 1c off the book; see Part 2 for the comparison with rows not picked, which is not significant at this size.

**Consequences.**
- **CFRTI1-BTC and its Kraken-only form:** the registered figures (PF 2.54 on n 36; PF 1.33 on n 32) are priced at a stale copy and are NOT tradable figures. The Nov 20 read still happens once; only its 6 second book-replay line and stale-ask count can support anything.
- **Every test priced from the quotes/ files or ladder.csv** is priced at the same delayed copy, including any earlier "Kalshi's quote lags spot" reading. R6, NC15, the hourly book recorder and the candle endpoints are not affected. Forensics is listing which files are which.
- **Part 2 (56cca50, with a band correction):** in the registered 5c to 30c band, the quote is 2c or more below the book on 4 of 15 picked three-venue rows (27 percent) against 7 percent of the 71 rows not picked; difference +20 points, interval -2 to +44. Kraken-only: 30 against 7 percent, interval -3 to +54. So the concentration on picked rows is "direction as predicted, no evidence at n = 15 and n = 10". (On the rule's own 3c to 30c band the difference was +26 points with an interval clear of zero; the registered band is the one quoted.) The conclusion rests on Part 1's mechanism, which does not depend on these rows.
- **CFRTI-B (approved, to be registered before Nov 20, 2 tries):** the clean version, signal and price both from the R6 order book, judged on the fill 6 seconds later. Two lines: B3 (three-venue spot; cannot go live, it answers whether spot information exists at all) and BK (Kraken-only; the only one that could run). Read once in the same Nov 20 sitting.
- **Verification Analyst on Part 1: PARTLY CONFIRM.** Its own code on two days reproduces the facts (16.3 polls a state; median 11.0 seconds between states; within 1c of the book 56.1 percent as stamped; best lag 5 to 7 seconds; both clocks within 58 ms). Not proven by the shift alone: which side is the old one. Forensics closed it with the trade tape as a third clock (TAPECLOCK1, effeb65; 412,603 cheap-side taker trades in 161 windows; no outcome, no reads): the R6 book is within 1c of the price takers paid 70 percent of the time at the trade's time and 81.5 percent one second later, while the quote recorder's ask is within 1c only 33 percent of the time and reaches its best (about 54 percent) only when read 7 to 8 seconds after the trade. **The quote is the old side.** (Not yet re-run by the analyst.) Also noted: agreement is worse nearer the close, and R6 book sizes change every 2 seconds at every level, so sizes are not depth truth without a check.
- **CFRTI-B development (registered 3ec1ddc7 first; nothing tuned):** B3 16 fills at 6 seconds, cash PF 1.39; BK 11 fills, PF 1.16. No evidence at n = 16 and n = 11. Held-out in the Nov 20 sitting.
- **CFRTI-K sample rule (in its registration, section 8, before any dispatch):** a one-request probe decides whether pre-cutover markets (closes before 2026-09-12) can be fetched in batches. If yes, development is the full set (about 280 requests). If no, development is fixed as post-cutover only (about 5,200 windows, about 95 requests) and the pre-cutover history is Block P: pulled and read once ONLY IF development shows cash PF 1.3 or better in each third with the haircut line above 1.0. The roughly 9,800 per-market requests are not dispatched otherwise. Job files: workers/desk_a_jobs/ on claude/idea-desk-a (0096dbe1). The development read waits for Forensics' CANDLEVSBOOK1.
- **CFRTI-K is re-specified before any scoring** to use only Kalshi's 1 minute candles: the perpetual's reference price and the Bitcoin 15 minute market's own candle for the decision price. Development becomes every window from the first day both series exist to before 2026-11-05 (on the order of ten thousand), judged on the research bar (cash PF 1.5 in each third, z 5.46), with the fill haircut as a second line. It needs one more staged pull (settled KXBTC15M market lists, then batched candles, about 320 requests). Forensics checks first that the candle ask close matches the book.
- **W-SPOTLEAD1 is not touched by the stale copy** (its edge test and fill both use the R6 book), but its signal is Coinbase 1 minute candles, a venue barred from live probes by the owner's ruling and with unread terms. It cannot go live as registered. The owner accepted the Coinbase and Bitstamp reads in research as an owner-accepted terms risk on Nov 12 (relayed by Main; research reads only; any live probe reading either venue still needs the owner's own popup naming that source), so the pause on new research requests is lifted.
- **The live bot:** it decides on the single-market read, not the list. Whether that read is a delayed copy too is unknown. Refinement answers it from the order rows since Nov 11 (the ask seen against the book snapshot a second later, by filled and not filled), by message to Main and the Head of R&D only. Any change is Main's and the owner's.

## Gold's look: what a paper record can be trusted for (Nov 12, Forensics' GOLDHAIRCUT1)

Recorded gold 15 minute books, closes Oct 29 to Nov 11, 400 to 330 seconds before the close, the 88c to 97c side, 292 windows (claude/forensics-desk, workers/forensics/GOLDHAIRCUT1_RESULT.md, 870de04; no outcome loaded; not yet checked by the Verification Analyst). Trust the PRICE to about 0.2c a contract; do not trust the COUNT. An immediate-or-cancel buy at the ask seen fills 59, 49 and 46 percent at about 2, 6 and 10 seconds; with +1c 94, 83 and 73 percent; 1 and 15 contracts the same. ### Stocks: six cards RETIRED UNREAD by arithmetic (Nov 12, 17:10Z; spend $0; nothing scored) [research]

PF 1.5 needs a net mean over standard deviation of about 0.162 a trade. For a rule that trades every day that is an annual Sharpe ratio near 2.6. The desk's arithmetic, with effect sizes from memory of the published work (so assumptions): S1 overnight index ETFs net about 0.05 (PF about 1.13); S2 single-name overnight clientele net negative after about 6.7bp a day of costs (PF about 0.9); S7 first 30 minutes predicting the last 30 net about 0.04 (PF about 1.1). No every-day rule on liquid US stocks built from a published anomaly can reach the bar after costs, and the bar does not change.

- **RETIRED UNREAD, cannot clear the bar after costs by arithmetic:** S1, S7 and S2's long-short line. **RETIRED UNREAD with them:** S3, S4, S5 (screens that could not pass). No data bought, no try spent. This supersedes the first-wave ruling in the section below.
- **S2's LONG LEG is HELD, not retired (changed at 17:11Z on the new desk's own prior of 3 to 8 percent):** it runs only if the pilot proves the listing venue's official open and close prints exist per name; cost about $5.60 (the one-month ranking pull, still allowed for this card only, plus daily bars for the 500 by the batch route); reported against a same-universe equal-weight overnight benchmark.
- **The forced-flow cards, ruled 17:12Z on the desk's page (docs/stocks/S6_S8_S9_PAGE.md on claude/stocks-desk; effect sizes from memory):** S6 RETIRED UNREAD (net PF about 1.0 to 1.15, about 300 events, cannot pass); S9 RETIRED UNREAD (PF about 1.0 to 1.2). S8 (closing auction imbalance) HELD: the desk first checks, free, on the exchanges' rule pages whether the order the card assumes can be entered after the imbalance is published; inside $25 it is LEAD-only (150 to 300 events against about 1,141 needed; one year of imbalance for 20 Nasdaq names re-estimated at $4.43).
- **S8 RETIRED UNREAD at 17:15Z** (docs/stocks/S8_REVIEW.md): the registered trade cannot be entered as written on NYSE (ordinary closing-order entry stops at 15:50, the moment the imbalance publishes); access to an offsetting order for a small account is unknown; the desk's own note puts the money at about $75 a year at $5,000 a trade. Not re-registered around an entry we cannot make.
- **State of the ten (updated 17:15Z):** S1, S3, S4, S5, S6, S7, S8, S9 retired unread (0 tries spent); S2 long leg held behind the $0.30 pilot (about $5.60 after it); S10 deferred. The desk's conclusion (docs/stocks/WHAT_A_STOCK_STRATEGY_NEEDS.md): a stock rule needs a net edge of about 8 to 22 basis points an event on 150 or more events a year with a forced counterparty; published public-data effects are a fraction of that after costs.

### Measured costs by family, first cut (Forensics, Nov 12 17:00Z)

kalshi-scalper/workers/forensics/FAMILY_COSTS_NOV12.md on claude/forensics-desk (af5fd4d). Recorded data only, no rule scored, zero tries. [research]

- The fee is most of the cost on every liquid family: about 1.5c to hold at mid prices, about 3c a round trip with a 1c spread.
- The taker fill haircut is general: a 1 contract order at the price seen fills 59 to 73 percent of the time a few seconds later on every 15 minute series measured (the five R6 series and the eleven non-crypto ones), 76 to 86 percent with a 1c higher limit.
- Non-crypto 15 minute series (20 hours of one weekday, a first reading): oil and silver trade like crypto (round trip 3.4c to 3.7c); the forex pairs are one maker 2c wide and one-sided a quarter to a third of the time near the close; copper, natural gas and USD/CAD cost 5c or more a round trip.
- Sports: NBA and NFL lines are tight and deep (round trip 4.2c to 4.8c). Politics: 3.4c overall, 6.3c for markets priced 20c to 80c.
- **Too costly, move on (five flags):** weather daily LOW brackets (round trip 11.5c on an 11c price); daily and weekly forex ladders, S&P 500 and Nasdaq dailies and Treasury auction markets (10c to 20c); monthly barriers on the smaller coins; NHL spread and total; combos as a two-way trade.
- Not held: events a year beyond the 15 minute and hourly series, sizes for weather and daily markets, settle speed for sports, anything on mentions.
- **How to price a paper trade (Forensics, 722ef2d, HOW_TO_PRICE_A_PAPER_TRADE.md):** the one page every card cites. Books, tapes and candles on KXBTC15M are current; any market list quote is a copy 0 to 11 s old; the perps list, perp candles and the bot's single-market read are "not known". Fee by price from 0.33c at 5c or 95c up to 1.75c at 50c. Replay on the book where one exists (first poll at least 2 s and 6 s later, real limit, walk the levels, a miss is no trade); where none exists cut the COUNT by 15 percent (+1c, 2 s), 23 percent (6 s), a third at the price seen. A paper record is good for price to about 0.2c, not count.
- **PERPSLIST1 (Forensics, registered 6b75d1f, result be76686): "no evidence at n = 4".** The held perps list data is too thin to say whether the list is a delayed copy (one 30 second file). Descriptive only: the perp candle's time stamp is the end of its minute, and list and candle agree closely 5 s before a minute end. Perp candle freshness stays "assumed, not measured" for PERPEQ1 and PERPBIN1 (no perp book or tape is recorded); for CASHSPREAD1 part B list freshness does not matter for spread levels. Re-read, same script and registration, about Nov 19 when about ten more 30 second files exist.
